I and the Center Party believe that water issues will become even more important in the future and can agree that new forms of cooperation are needed because water, like weather and wind, has a tendency not to relate to either municipal or national borders. The report well describes how regions and the state through the County Administrative Board will work together within the framework of the regional plans that the Center Party wants and which will now be expanded with my own county of Halland. Madam President, I would like Halland's rivers have all been taught in school, but they are not only Halland's. The sources lie further inland, and they are what are commonly called river basins — or districts, as they are called in the water management investigation. In Halland's rivers and many of their side-flows there is also part of the renewable electricity generation that I started by talking about. Small-scale hydropower has been a follow-on in the Civil Committee. It is not the first time that the government has received announcements that the testing of individual production facilities should also be done on the basis of what social benefits they are doing for our electricity supply and what they have meant to society further back in time. The measures required by the authorities today by hydropower owners lead to the decomposition of fully functioning dams and turbines. The Center Party wants the assessments to be based on the conditions on the ground. Which fish is expected to hike, and where are the hiking routes to be built in cases where they are really needed? The assessment must also be based on proportionality in terms of benefits and profitability and that it does not adversely affect the overall production of electricity. Someone may object that compared to the fully expanded hydroelectric power in the large Norrland rivers, a small part is produced small-scalely. But it's the control force you want. As mentioned above, electricity should preferably be produced close to where it will be used. The planning objective of the small hydroelectric power plant is that the measures must not lead to a loss of production of more than one and a half terawatt hours. So we should not increase production in small-scale hydropower, but limit the reduction. Any derogation must therefore be available to hydropower owners. That is not the case today. That is why we believe that the test is not made on the basis of the law that we passed here and that it must therefore be paused. In March, the Riksdag decided that the timetable must be revised so that the trials started are completed according to the programme of measures that has not yet been completed. There is a risk that the trials that start later will have different environmental conditions than those where you have been out early with your applications. The Riksdag has therefore on several occasions called on the Government to either stop the trials or ensure that they are done according to the will expressed by the Riksdag – that the rules should be simpler and the measures proportionate. I would therefore like to support the proposal and the rejection of reservation 4 and therefore also to support reservation 5 on the greening of hydroelectric power.
i-AVcV4ZNa8Yh31iniusQ9ea	Madam President, I would like Sometimes it is said that without the car Sweden stops, and I believe that is to some extent true. It could also be said that small hydro power is just as important for our energy supply as the car is for our transport. Without small-scale hydropower, we get even bigger deficits, especially when there is no wind or the sun does not shine. One result of the large deficit is the much higher prices of energy based on the now reduced supply. The fact that this issue has not been given more political explosive power than it has been given is a mystery to me. A price which in some cases has been raised by several hundred per cent should in fact be the only one to be discussed. Of course, we Christian Democrats advocate that nuclear power should be allowed to develop further in Sweden, but we also need other types of energy production, such as small-scale hydropower. Using energy in our smaller streams is something we have done for many centuries. Early on, we settled at waterfalls where the mechanical force could make life easier for us, for example by driving saws and hammers. Hydropower has been and is a cornerstone of the development of our society, today thanks to the electricity generation that we so badly need. Without electricity generation from small hydro power, we would have had even greater problems with the energy supply. The Swedish Parliament has on several occasions made decisions with the meaning that small-scale hydropower should be protected. In spite of this, we have experienced over time that authorities continue to make demands on the destruction of small-scale hydropower in connection with the trials that take place on a basis other than what the Riksdag wants to see. My questions will then be: How will we get our authorities to follow the will of the Swedish Parliament? How are we going to get the Swedish Government to operate in the direction that the Swedish Parliament has expressed innumerable times? Why does the government not see to it that our authorities have the current basis for making decisions? Instead, the time for processing in the Government Office will be so long that you will have to use the material that the Riksdag has already decided to replace. I find it sad that we have authorities and a government that considers itself to be above the democratically demonstrated will of the Swedish Parliament. Since the government and the authorities are not responding to Parliament's wishes, we must simply continue to talk about Parliament's will, as we are doing here today. In March 2022, the Swedish Parliament, on the initiative of the committee, issued a notice to the government that the timetable of the national hydropower review plan should be adjusted if the government's examination of the water authorities' action programme is not completed when the next review team is due to submit its applications. We Christian Democrats believe that this was a step in the right direction, but in our opinion there are other outstanding issues that need to be reviewed before the reconsiderations can continue. Not least, something needs to be done because of the legal uncertainty that exists, which stems from different authorities in different parts of the country making differing assessments. In this context, we would like to emphasise that the removal of dams does not necessarily improve biodiversity or the local environment. In many cases a dam may in fact have created conditions for both fish, mussels and birds. We must realise that we need small-scale hydropower and that, as a society, we should seize its potential and cultural heritage. With these words, I am in favour of reservation number 7.
i-Hd5xprhi9ixHVV8HL3YC3b	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen. Thank you for staying this late hour! Water is a prerequisite for all life that we feel it – a vital resource that we all too often take for granted. The fact that water is crucial to our food production, electricity supply, biodiversity and recreation makes an enormous number of interests struggling for their very space. With the ever more severe effects of climate change, we see that water is becoming scarce, while floods threaten our cities. Dry periods threaten groundwater levels, and already in April Gotland, among other things, imposes restrictions on how and to what water resources may be used. Unfortunately, this is a reality that we must face and adapt to. Sweden was planned and built when the conditions were completely different. It is now up to us in Parliament to ensure that water resources are protected and used where they are most useful. Today we have to deal with 140 requests for exercise from the general exercise period. The large number of requests shows that many of our colleagues are aware of how we can make the best use of our water resources and make proposals on this. But it is not just climate change and great domestic competition for our water resources that need to be addressed. Russia's unjustified war against Ukraine also affects us in terms of how we must prioritise our limited water resources. I will come back to that. Madam President, I would like All my colleagues have mentioned this: Hydropower in Sweden is the power generation that historically and nowadays constitutes the backbone of our supply of fossil-free and adjustable electricity. We have historically been good at harnessing the local natural resources that hydropower offers. Society and companies have developed around watercourses throughout Sweden. Hydroelectric power stations have been cared for and appreciated not only for decades but for centuries by those who live close to them and have seen how they can offer jobs and secure electricity supply. Of course, small-scale hydropower must have modern environmental conditions, but the environmental permit process must be balanced and must not make the continued operation and efficiency of small-scale hydropower impossible. This should be self-evident, and the Riksdag has been over-clear about how we want the government and the authorities to act. Unfortunately, it has not always proved to be as obvious for those who are going to administer the environmental processes. Nor has the government shown any willingness to address the challenges that threaten the existence of small-scale hydropower. Due to this reluctance, the Riksdag has addressed a number of announcements to the Government. Let me give you a little historical flashback. The first announcement came in 2016 from the Civil Committee, which asked the government to return to the Riksdag with a regulatory framework for permit processes that are flexible and adapted to small-scale operations. The Energy Agreement 2016 resulted in a bill 2018. It concluded, inter alia, that the authorisation testing carried out by the authorities should have a national holistic approach, balancing the need for measures to improve water quality with the need for national efficient access to electricity from hydropower. It was a bill that gave some hope for a more balanced management of small-scale hydropower, but unfortunately it turned out that the bill, which the Riksdag supported, did not get the desired impact. So in 2020, another announcement, this time by the Committee on Economic Affairs, focused on reducing regulatory burdens, respect for property rights and efficient and affordable environmental measures, was issued. The desired result failed again, and therefore a new announcement came in September 2021, this time from the Committee on the Environment and Agriculture. The whole Parliament has therefore been involved in this. The government's mills grind slowly. The last two announcements are still pending, so, Madam President, there is still hope that the government will finally listen to the Riksdag. While the announcements are being handled, the responsible authorities continue their efforts to implement the national plans for the greening of small-scale hydropower. This creates great uncertainty and concern among the owners of this decentralised electricity production. That is why, as recently as March this year, the Committee on Civil Liberties considered that a further announcement was necessary – this time in order to get the water authorities to adjust the national plan for the re-evaluation of hydro power as long as the action programme for the management cycle 2021 – 2027 was not established. It remains to be seen, Madam President, whether this time the government is following the decision taken by this Parliament. After today's statement in Expressen about how the government views announcements, the hope is not very great. Madam President, I would like One might think that announcements on the subject of small hydropower are now enough. But we must act when the world around us changes. And that is why, Madam President, there is also today a announcement to the government with a clear statement that small-scale hydropower is a resource that we must protect and develop. In substance, this announcement has the same meaning as the previous one, namely that the government should take the necessary measures and take the necessary initiatives to ensure that regulation at EU level does not unjustifiably hamper or render the operation of small-scale hydroelectric plants impossible. What has now been added, in addition to the arguments put forward earlier, is the important role of small hydroelectric power as decentralised electricity production at a time when external forces are threatening our neighbourhood. With Russia's invasion of Ukraine and Russia's constant threat to those who support Ukraine's struggle for its freedom, the importance of robust, decentralised electricity production has become increasingly evident. Small-scale hydropower is important from a total defence perspective. This must be included in the assessment when the environmental impact of small hydropower is weighed against the societal benefit. The Moderate Defence Committee's motion highlights that small hydropower, with its role in total defence, should not only be protected but also be developed and strengthened. As a result of the Energy Agreement, it was established that the greening measures would not entail more than 1.5 terawatt-hour reduction in electricity generation. With awareness of what is happening in our near world, the question is whether this figure should not be changed to become a planned increase of at least 1.5 terawatt hours. Small-scale hydropower is a socially important activity and must be managed accordingly.
